** Old HTTP code

Decided in the end to have the client make multiple requests using
existing endpoints instead.

#+begin_src c++
      void HandleClientRequest(HTTPServer *server, HTTPRequest &&request, const std::vector<std::string> &path_parts, size_t command_index) {
          if (request.content_type != HTTP_JSON_CONTENT_TYPE) {
              server->SendResponse(request, HTTPResponse::UnsupportedMediaType(request));
              return;
          }

          if (!request.content_type_charset.empty() && request.content_type_charset != HTTP_UTF8_CHARSET) {
              server->SendResponse(request, HTTPResponse::UnsupportedMediaType(request));
              return;
          }

          nlohmann::json j_content;
          try {
              j_content = nlohmann::json::parse(request.body);
          } catch (nlohmann::json::exception &exc) {
              server->SendResponse(request, HTTPResponse::BadRequest(request, "JSON parse error: %s", exc.what()));
              return;
          }

          ClientRequest crequest;
          try {
              crequest = j_content.get<ClientRequest>();
          } catch (nlohmann::json::exception &exc) {
              server->SendResponse(request, HTTPResponse::BadRequest(request, "JSON format error: %s", exc.what()));
              return;
          }

          BeebWindow *beeb_window;
          if (crequest.window_name.empty()) {
              beeb_window = BeebWindows::FindMRUBeebWindow();
          } else {
              beeb_window = BeebWindows::FindBeebWindowByName(crequest.window_name);
              if (!beeb_window) {
                  server->SendResponse(request, HTTPResponse::ServiceUnavailable());
                  return;
              }
          }

          ClientResponse cresponse = beeb_window->HandleClientRequest(crequest);

          j_content = cresponse;

          HTTPResponse response;
          response.content_type = HTTP_JSON_CONTENT_TYPE;
          response.content_type_charset = HTTP_UTF8_CHARSET;
          response.SetContentString(j_content.dump(4));

          server->SendResponse(request, std::move(response));
      }
#+end_src

** Section for docs

#+begin_src markdown
  # b2client

  b2client is a command line program intended to make it a bit more
  convenient to use the HTTP API from a Makefile or other build system.
  No need to specify a URL, pick the right encoding, call curl multiple
  times - b2client handles all of that for you.

  Its location is OS-dependent:

  ,* Windows: `b2client.exe` is in the distribution zip file
  ,* macOS: `b2client` is in the b2 Debug app bundle, so it'll probably
  be somewhere like `/Applications/b2 Debug.app/Contents/MacOS/b2client`
  ,* Linux: `b2client` is wherever `b2` is, either the build path or
    install path

  There's a help summary available by running it with `b2client --help`,
  but the basic options are these:

  ,* `--0 FILE`, `--1 FILE` - load `FILE` into the given drive
  ,* `--0-in-memory, `--1-in-memory` - in conjunction with `--0` or
    `--1`, load the corresponding file as an in-memory disk image
  ,* `--config CONFIG` - select the config named `CONFIG`, and do a
    power-on reset
  ,* `--boot` - do a power-on reset and auto-boot the BBC

  These will try to find a running copy of b2 and send it the
  appropriate requests.

  If b2client couldn't send the request, or the running copy of b2
  rejected the request, b2client will end with a non-zero exit code and
  a message on stderr.
#+end_src

* Trace file binary format?

For possible use with query tools that I haven't written yet. Rather
than load in and parse a huge pile of ASCII, might as well load in and
parse a (smaller, and ideally quicker to save) pile of binary instead.

Host events:

- R6522::IRQ_EVENT :: VIA IRQ
- R6522::TIMER_TICK_EVENT :: VIA timer tick
- SN76489::WRITE_EVENT :: SN76489 write
- SN76489::UPDATE_EVENT :: SN76489 update (currently compiled out)
- Trace::WRITE_ROMSEL_EVENT :: write ROMSEL
- Trace::WRITE_ACCCON_EVENT :: write ACCCON
- Trace::SET_MAPPER_REGION_EVENT :: set ROM mapper region

Parasite events:

- Trace::PARASITE_BOOT_MODE_EVENT :: indicate parasite boot mode

Common events:

- BBCMicro::INSTRUCTION_EVENT :: 6502 instruction
- Trace::STRING_EVENT :: generic string event
- Trace::DISCONTINUITY_EVENT :: bridge gap too large for delta bitfield
- TUBE_WRITE_STATUS_EVENT :: Tube event
- TUBE_WRITE_FIFO1_EVENT :: Tube event
- TUBE_READ_FIFO1_EVENT :: Tube event
- TUBE_WRITE_FIFO2_EVENT :: Tube event
- TUBE_READ_FIFO2_EVENT :: Tube event
- TUBE_WRITE_FIFO3_EVENT :: Tube event
- TUBE_READ_FIFO3_EVENT :: Tube event
- TUBE_WRITE_FIFO4_EVENT :: Tube event
- TUBE_READ_FIFO4_EVENT :: Tube event

All have a struct attached, currently all pack1, hopefully.

Each one has a name assigned - so no need for explicit IDs, provided
each name is unique! Store the mapping somehow and off it goes.

A sizeof check would be nice, just to trap any obvious changes in the
format. For the versioning, have a file version number, which could
cover changes to event header and event contents too. For event
contents alone, adjusting the name will suffice.

** Format ideas

All values are little endian.

*** Header

| Offset | Type        | Name               | What                             |
|--------+-------------+--------------------+----------------------------------|
|      0 | char[8]     |                    | Header - must be "b2trace\x0"    |
|      8 | uint8_t     | version            | Version number                   |
|      9 | uint8_t     | num_events         | Number of event types            |
|     13 | uint64_t    | freq               | Cycles per second                |
|     14 | uint8_t     | romsel             | Initial ROMSEL value             |
|     15 | uint8_t     | acccon             | Initial ACCCON value             |
|     16 | uint8_t[16] | regions            | Initial ROM mapper regions       |
|     32 | char[8]     | host_cpu           | Host CPU type                    |
|     40 | char[8]     | parasite_cpu       | Parasite CPU type                |
|     48 | uint8_t     | parasite_boot_mode | Initial parasite boot mode state |
|--------+-------------+--------------------+----------------------------------|
|     49 |             |                    |                                  |

Probably pad to 64 or 128 bytes...?

TODO: SaveTrace takes a BBCMicroType pointer, and it calls
=get_mem_big_page_tables_fn=. This might be a faff to reproduce in a
fully offline tool.

CPU types are:

- =none= :: No CPU (only relevant for parasite)
- =defined= :: 6502 defined instructions only
- =nmos= :: 6502, NMOS
- =cmos= :: 65C02, CMOS
- =rockw= :: Rockwell 65C02

*** Event types

Information about each event type ID. There are header:num_events of
these.

| Offset | Type      | Name      | What                            |
|--------+-----------+-----------+---------------------------------|
|      0 | uint8_t   | type:id   | Type id                         |
|      1 | uint16_t  | type:size | Size of data, or 0 if per-event |
|      3 | uint8_t[] | type:name | 0-terminated event type name    |
|--------+-----------+-----------+---------------------------------|
| 3+N    |           |           |                                 |

*** Event header

All events have a fixed 2-byte header.

| Offset | Type    | Name        | What                                                  |
|--------+---------+-------------+-------------------------------------------------------|
|      0 | uint8_t | event:type  | Type id (matching type id from the event types table) |
|      1 | uint8_t | event:flags | Flags (see below)                                     |
|--------+---------+-------------+-------------------------------------------------------|
|      2 |         |             |                                                       |

Events with a =type:size= field of 0 have an additional 2-byte header
indicating the size of the event.

| Offset | Type     | Name       | What                    |
|--------+----------+------------+-------------------------|
|      0 | uint16_t | event:size | Size (in bytes) of data |
|--------+----------+------------+-------------------------|
|      2 |          |            |                         |

=event:flags= is laid out as follows:

:   7   6   5   4   3   2   1   0
: +---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+
: | c | src   | time_delta        |
: +---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+

=time_delta= is cycle count since previous event.

=src= is event source, as follows:

- 0 :: undefined
- 1 :: host
- 2 :: parasite
- 3 :: reserved

=c= is set if the event was cancelled. It still counts as a previous
event for future time_delta purposes, but its contents must be
ignored.

*** R6522::IRQ_EVENT

Name: =R6522IRQEvent=

The 6522 generated an IRQ.

| Offset | Type    | Name | What                     |
|--------+---------+------+--------------------------|
|      0 | uint8_t | id   | VIA id
|      1 | uint8_t | ifr  | IFR value                |
|      2 | uint8_t | ier  | IER value                |
|--------+---------+------+--------------------------|
|      3 |         |      |                          |

Current VIA ids are:

- 0 :: system VIA
- 1 :: user VIA

*** R6522::TIMER_TICK_EVENT

Name: =R6522TimerTick=

A timer tick occurred. This is a high frequency event...

| Offset | Type     | Name   | What              |
|--------+----------+--------+-------------------|
|      0 | uint16_t | new_t1 | New T1 value      |
|      2 | uint16_t | new_t2 | New T2 value      |
|      4 | uint8_t  | flags  | Flags (see below) |
|--------+----------+--------+-------------------|
|      5 |          |        |                   |

=flags= is laid out as follows:

:   7   6   5   4   3   2   1   0
: +---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+
: | 0 | 0 |t1 |t2 | id            |
: +---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+

=t1= set if t1 ticked.

=t2= set if t2 ticked.

=id= is VIA id - see above.

It's possible (but unlikely) that neither tick flag will be set..

*** SN76489::WRITE_EVENT

Name: =SN76489WriteEvent=

A byte was written to the SN76489.

| Offset | Type     | Name  | What          |
|--------+----------+-------+---------------|
|      0 | uint8_t  | value | Value written |
|      2 | uint16_t | reg   | Register info |
|--------+----------+-------+---------------|
|      3 |          |       |               |

=reg= is laid out as follows:

:   f   e   d   c   b   a   9   8   7   6   5   4   3   2   1   0
: +---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+
: |   |   |   | reg       | value                                 |
: +---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+---+

=reg= is register index.

=value= is entire new value for register.

*** SN76489::UPDATE_EVENT

Name: =SN76489UpdateEvent=

TODO: includes some non-packed struct values! Need to sort this out...

*** Trace::WRITE_ROMSEL_EVENT

Name: =_write_romsel=

Write ROMSEL value. 

| Offset | Type    | Name  | What                 |
|--------+---------+-------+----------------------|
|      0 | uint8_t | value | ROMSEL value written |
|--------+---------+-------+----------------------|
|      1 |         |       |                      |

*** Trace::WRITE_ACCCON_EVENT

Name: =_write_acccon=

Write ACCCON value.

| Offset | Type    | Name  | What                 |
|--------+---------+-------+----------------------|
|      0 | uint8_t | value | ACCCON value written |
|--------+---------+-------+----------------------|
|      1 |         |       |                      |

*** Trace::SET_MAPPER_REGION_EVENT

Name: =_set_mapper_region=

Set ROM mapper region.

| Offset | Type    | Name  | What            |
|--------+---------+-------+-----------------|
|      0 | uint8_t | value | Region selected |
|--------+---------+-------+-----------------|
|      1 |         |       |                 |

*** Trace::PARASITE_BOOT_MODE_EVENT

Name: =_parasite_boot_mode=

Set parasite boot mode.

| Offset | Type    | Name  | What                    |
|--------+---------+-------+-------------------------|
|      0 | uint8_t | value | Parasite boot mode flag |
|--------+---------+-------+-------------------------|
|      1 |         |       |                         |

*** BBCMicro::INSTRUCTION_EVENT

Name: =Instruction=

Indicate instruction was executed.

The logic for this is a bit fiddly, and the 6502 type plays a part.

| Offset | Type     | Name   | What              |
|--------+----------+--------+-------------------|
|      0 | uint8_t  | a      | Accumulato        |
|      1 | uint8_t  | x      | X register        |
|      2 | uint8_t  | y      | Y register        |
|      3 | uint8_t  | p      | P register        |
|      4 | uint8_t  | data   | Data value        |
|      5 | uint8_t  | opcode | Opcode executed   |
|      6 | uint8_t  | s      | S register        |
|      7 | uint16_t | pc     | PC register       |
|      9 | uint16_t | ad     | Effective address |
|     11 | uint16_t | ia     | Indirect address  |
|--------+----------+--------+-------------------|
|     13 |          |        |                   |

Probably want to preprocess this a bit. Don't just reproduce what gets
recorded to the trace buffer - do a bit of preprocessing so that
what's in the data is more like an encoded version of what gets saved
to the text file. The logic is in the emulator already, so no need to
have it in the tool as well.

Need to define some representation for the mapper regions and so on:

Addressing state bits for host:

| Flag     | Bits | What              |
|----------+------+-------------------|
| 0-9, a-f |    4 | Sideways ROM      |
| n        |    1 | ANDY              |
| h        |    1 | HAZEL             |
| s        |    1 | Shadow            |
| A-P      |    4 | ROM mapper region |

Addressing state bits for parasite:

| Flag | Bits |
|------+------|
| r    |    1 |

Ugh. But, since it's already over 1 byte, might as well just store the
2 chars directly.

: H        1597  $da4a`o : sta  $0200,X [$02d5`m]   ff d5 7e ff NvdIzC (ff)

(Cycle count, PC, opcode, operand, actual address, a, x, y, s, p, data)

| Offset | Type     | What              |
|--------+----------+-------------------|
|      0 | uint8_t  | a                 |
|      1 | uint8_t  | x                 |
|      2 | uint8_t  | y                 |
|      3 | uint8_t  | p                 |
|      4 | uint8_t  | data              |
|      5 | uint8_t  | opcode            |
|      6 | uint8_t  | s                 |
|      7 | uint16_t | pc                |
|      9 | char[2]  | pc addr flags     |
|     11 | uint16_t | operand           |
|     13 | uint16_t | actual addr       |
|     15 | char[2]  | actual addr flags |
|--------+----------+-------------------|
|     17 |          |                   |

*** Trace::STRING_EVENT

Name: =_string=

Add an arbitrary string to the log.

TODO: The string length is included in the header, but the string is
0-terminated anyway. Is this really necessary?

| Offset | Type   | Name | What                |
|--------+--------+------+---------------------|
|      0 | char[] | str  | 0-terminated string |

*** Trace::DISCONTINUITY_EVENT

Name: =_discontinuity=

Indicate a passage of time too large for the =time_delta= bitfield.

TODO: the new cycle count is an absolute value, not relative. Does it
have to be like that?

| Offset | Type     | Name     | What            |
|--------+----------+----------+-----------------|
|      0 | uint64_t | new_time | New cycle count |
|--------+----------+----------+-----------------|
|      8 |          |          |                 |

* Challenger Bad Track Format

Repro steps:

1. insert .ddd

2. =*CAT=

3. =LOAD "P0"=

4. =*CAT=

One oddity noted: disk drive ends up on track 3 after this. Is that
intentional? Shouldn't it be attempting to return to track 0 to read
the catalogue?

?&FFFF=0:*CAT
LOAD"P0"
*CAT

* SCSI

Existing ADFS disassemblies:

https://github.com/dominicbeesley/ADFS-multi-target

https://mdfs.net/Info/Comp/BBC/ADFS/

https://github.com/hoglet67/ADFS130

JGH's has some SCSI code notes, and addresses that look like they
match the ADFS from MOS 3.20.

** Bleargh (part 1/N)

Start of command $08:

H     1417275  $833a`d : pla  [$01ef`m]           08 00 05 ef nvdizC (08)
H     1417279  $833b`d : bit  $cc [$00cc`m]       08 00 05 ef nvdiZC (22)
H     1417282  $833d`d : rts  [$01f1`m]           08 00 05 f1 nvdiZC (83)
H     1417288  $8341`d : bvs  $8349               08 00 05 f1 nvdiZC (00)
H     1417290  $8343`d : sta  $fc40 [$fc40`i]     08 00 05 f1 nvdiZC (08)
H     1417295  SCSI - WriteData: Phase=Command; value=$08
H     1417295  SCSI - Command +0 (6 left): $08

#+begin_src beebasm
  .L806F PHP
  .L8070 LDA &FC41        ;; Get SCSI status
         STA &CC          ;; Save this value
         LDA &FC41        ;; Get SCSI status
         CMP &CC          ;; Compare with previous status
         BNE L8070        ;; Loop until status stays same
         PLP
         RTS
#+end_src

On exit from L8332: V=io, N=cd

#+begin_src beebasm
  IF HD_SCSI
  .L8332 PHA              ;; Save A
  .L8333 JSR L806F        ;; Get SCSI status
         AND #&20         ;; Check REQUEST
         BEQ L8333        ;; Loop until REQUEST set
         PLA              ;; Restore A
         BIT &CC          ;; Set flags from SCSI status
         RTS

  .L833E JSR L8332        ;; Wait until SCSI ready
         BVS L8349        ;; SCSI not responding, drop return and return result=UNKNOWN
         STA &FC40
         LDA #&00		;; Return Ok
         RTS
  .L8349 PLA              ;; Drop return address
         PLA
         JMP L81AD        ;; Jump to get result and return
#+end_src

#+begin_src beebasm
  .L814A LDA (&B0),Y      ;; Get a command block byte
  .L814C JSR L833E        ;; Send to SCSI data port
         JSR L8332        ;; Wait until SCSI busy
         BPL L8159        ;; If SCSI says enough command
         BVS L8159        ;; bytes sent, jump ahead
         INY              ;; Keep sending command block
         BNE L814A        ;; until SCSI says 'stop!'
#+end_src

** Bleargh (part 2/N) - SUPERFORM reads incorrect drive parameters

Parameters look correct...

m_buffer is filled correctly...

Correct bytes are read from $fc40...!

H      386925  $81b3`d : lda  $fc40 [$fc40`i]     00 01 00 f0 nVdIZC (00)
H      386930  SCSI - ReadData: Phase=Read
H      386930  SCSI - ReadData: m_offset=0 (0x0); m_length=22 (0x16)
H      386931  $81b6`d : jsr  $8332 [$01ef`m]     00 01 00 ee nVdIZC (81)
...
H      386995  $81b9`d : tay                      00 01 00 f0 nVdIZC (20)
H      386997  $81ba`d : jsr  $806f [$01ef`m]     00 01 00 ee nVdIZC (81)
H      387034  $81bd`d : and  #$01                00 01 00 f0 nVdIZC (01)
H      387036  $81bf`d : beq  $81b0               00 01 00 f0 nVdIZC (01)


#+begin_src beebasm
  .L81B0 JSR L8332        ;; Wait for SCSI data ready
         LDA &FC40        ;; Get result byte
         JSR L8332        ;; Wait for SCSI data ready
         TAY              ;; Save result
         JSR L806F        ;; Get SCSI status
         AND #&01
         BEQ L81B0        ;; Loop to try to get result again
         TYA              ;; Get result back
         LDX &FC40        ;; Get second result byte
         BEQ L81CA        ;; OK, jump to return result
         JMP L82A5        ;; Return result=&7F
#+end_src

** Test process

Copy BeebEm disk images:

#+begin_example
  c:\temp\hard_disk_images\
  copy C:\tom\not-my\beebem-windows\UserData\DiscIms\scsi0.* .
#+end_example

Run SUPERFORM. Use option C to select drive parameters.

** Drive parameter notes

https://mdfs.net/Docs/Comp/Disk/Format/ADFS

But then, results of superform...?

| # tracks | result     | size |
|----------+------------+------|
|        1 | Bad FS map |      |
|        2 | Bad FS map |      |
|        3 | Bad FS map |      |
|        4 | Bad FS map |      |
|        5 | Disc full  |      |
|        6 | Disc full  |      |
|        7 | ok         |      |
|        8 | ok         |      |
|        9 | ok         |      |
|       10 | ok         |      |

For 7 tracks:

2 sectors free space map
5 sectors directory

7 tracks = 7*33 = 231 ($e7) sectors = 59136

-2 for free map = 229
-5 for $ = 222
-5 for $.FORMAT = 217

217 ($d9) * 256 = 55552

But *FREE says 9 sectors free, $5e (94) sectors used... 103 total?

Looks like SUPERFORM reserves 128 sectors (~4 tracks) - for bad sector
remapping purposes? See lines 3990 and 4000. So that explains that.
The disk just has to be larger than you expect. Worth noting in the
docs.

* Serial Stuff

** Links

BBC tape input circuit: https://stardot.org.uk/forums/viewtopic.php?f=3&t=22935

BeebWiki docs: https://beebwiki.mdfs.net/Serial_ULA

** Baud rates

The notional ULA baud rates are all divisors of 19200 (or multiples of
75). 600 baud isn't an option.

According to BeebWiki, the serial ULA provides some multiple of a
16/13 MHz clock for the ACIA. So presumably the actual ACIA clock Hz
is as per the column here.

| ULA baud | ULA div | 16/13 ACIA clock Hz? |
|----------+---------+----------------------+
|    19200 |       1 |   1230769.2307692308 |
|     9600 |       2 |    615384.6153846154 |
|     4800 |       4 |    307692.3076923077 |
|     2400 |       8 |   153846.15384615384 |
|     1200 |      16 |    76923.07692307692 |
|      300 |      64 |    19230.76923076923 |
|      150 |     128 |    9615.384615384615 |
|       75 |     256 |    4807.692307692308 |

The ACIA itself applies a further divider: /1, /16 or /64.

Closest approximation to 16/13 in b2 is going to be 4/13. For ACIA
dividers /16 and /64, no problem. For /1... well... hmm... but it
should be ok for any useful bitrate value? The BBC is going to have to
work hard enough to hit 76.8 Kbaud (or 76923.076923 Hz as it must
actually be), so the cases b2 can never handle properly are surely
unimportant.

| ULA baud | ULA div |  real ACIA Hz? |     /16 baud |     /64 baud |       b2 ACIA Hz |     /16 baud |     /64 baud |
|----------+---------+----------------+--------------+--------------+------------------+--------------+--------------|
|    19200 |       1 | 1230769.230769 | 76923.076923 | 19230.769231 | 307692.307692 :( | 76923.076923 | 19230.769231 |
|     9600 |       2 |  615384.615385 | 38461.538462 |  9615.384615 | 307692.307692 :( | 38461.538462 |  9615.384615 |
|     4800 |       4 |  307692.307692 | 19230.769231 |  4807.692308 |    307692.307692 | 19230.769231 |  4807.692308 |
|     2400 |       8 |  153846.153846 |  9615.384615 |  2403.846154 |    153846.153846 |  9615.384615 |  2403.846154 |
|     1200 |      16 |   76923.076923 |  4807.692308 |  1201.923077 |     76923.076923 |  4807.692308 |  1201.923077 |
|      300 |      64 |   19230.769231 |  1201.923077 |   300.480769 |     19230.769231 |  1201.923077 |   300.480769 |
|      150 |     128 |    9615.384615 |   600.961538 |   150.240385 |      9615.384615 |   600.961538 |   150.240385 |
|       75 |     256 |    4807.692308 |   300.480769 |    75.120192 |      4807.692308 |   300.480769 |    75.120192 |


** Random notes

In RS423 mode, ~DCD=0 (DCD=1), so the receiver is always active. With
nothing connected, valid characters are never received, so there's no
overrun errors. But if you read the data register, a parity error or
framing error may occur.

Regarding the ULA register settings in tape mode (see
https://beebwiki.mdfs.net/Serial_ULA):

#+begin_quote
The ULA itself ignores bits 5 to 3 and generates a receive clock of
19.2 kHz when bit 6 is clear. (The SERPROC also disregards bits 5 and
4 but reassigns bit 3 for another purpose, see below.) When saving,
bits 2 to 0 as well as the ACIA clock divider govern the baud rate, as
in serial mode, but again the MOS only adjusts the divider.
#+end_quote

* CMake vs Info.plist

Trying to get configuration-dependent bundle identifier.

Target properties can't use generator expressions, so there's no easy
way to put configuration-specific data into the Info.plist file, nor
specify a configuration-specific Info.plist template.

There seems to be no way to hook into the Info.plist generation
generally.

A POST_BUILD tool nearly works, but the timing of the plist generation
step isn't consistent, so there's the risk of changes being
overwritten. (And Info.plist generation doesn't happen on every
build. Another source of inconsistency.)

** Annoying plist tools

The macOS plist tools are all pretty infuriating, too! defaults won't
read from files, and PlistBuddy and plutil make it very hard to update
items that may or may not already exist - a common occurrence, since
the bundle's Info.plist isn't updated on every build, but the
POST_BUILD tools are. (Setting the items fails if they don't exist.
Adding the items fails if they do. And deleting the items fails if
they don't. But that's just details.

So the template Info.plist includes GUIDs that can be replaced
textually using sed. sed doesn't mind if it has nothing to do.

** Trying to make it work as-is

After a lot of back and forth: seems the build process doesn't
actually use the specified Info.plist template directly. It copies it
somewhere at configure time, and then acts on the copy. First pass
attempt at making this work: have the build process copy it to the
same place the configure process does.

* Testing with SDL3+sdl2-compat

`dependencies` in the working copy is the ideal folder for this.

: git clone https://github.com/libsdl-org/sdl2-compat
: git clone https://github.com/libsdl-org/SDL

For sdl2-compat, head revision is probably good.

For SDL:

: git checkout release-3.2.x

SDL3 can just build itself. `add_subdirectory` somewhere in the
CMakeLists.txt.

: add_subdirectory(${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/dependencies/SDL)

sdl2-compat is similar. But note that it generates some SDL2::
targets, so remove anything that would generate those. CMake will moan
about this stuff if anything goes wrong, so trial and error will
work.

: add_subdirectory(${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/dependencies/sdl2-compat)

* Symbols vs DSOs

Open issues:

- If you specify an address suffix for a symbol file, the symbols will
  be visible only when that suffix applies. For sideways ROMs, this is
  (probably) exactly what you want, because the symbols will only be
  relevant when the ROM is paged in, including those in main RAM.

  But this might not be what you want if you've got one symbol file
  that straddles multiple paging regions in main memory. Like, even if
  you've got some symbols that are only valid when shadow RAM is paged
  in (say), when it's paged out, you'll still want the symbols in the
  $0000-$3000 region to be valid, since that region is the same in
  both cases.

  (Something similar should also apply to the ROM mappers that have
  fixed regions.)

  This is fixable. There's just no info in the code anywhere about
  which addresses are affected by which suffix codes

- The symbol parsing doesn't take the ` syntax into account, so you
  always end up with the DSO for the group the symbol is in

- As well as typing in the address suffix, there should have some
  variant of the page override UI for selecting it. Would be nice to
  have 3 options: type it in, select it from a generic page override
  UI (accommodating every possible option), or select it from a
  type-specific page override UI (that's dependent on the current BBC
  Micro type, as per the ones seen at the top of each bit of debug UI)

DSO test:

#+begin_src c++
  #include <shared/system.h>
  #include <beeb/conf.h>

  #if BBCMICRO_DEBUGGER

  #include <inttypes.h>
  #include <beeb/type.h>
  #include <shared/testing.h>
  #include <shared/debug.h>
  #include <shared/log.h>

  static bool GetOverrideMask(uint32_t *flags, const std::shared_ptr<const BBCMicroType> &type, int control, uint32_t override, uint32_t value) {
      switch (control) {
      default:
          ASSERT(false);
          [[fallthrough]];
      case -1:
          ,*flags = 0;
          return true;

      case 0:
          ,*flags = override;
          break;

      case 1:
          ,*flags = override | value;
          break;
      }

      if (override & type->dso_mask) {
          return true;
      } else {
          return false;
      }
  }

  LOG_EXTERN(TESTING);

  static uint32_t TestDSOs(std::shared_ptr<const BBCMicroType> type) {
      uint32_t num_dsos = 0;

      int rom, region, andy, hazel, shadow, os;
      std::string suffix;
      uint32_t parsed_dso;

      AddTestFailFn([&](const TestFailArgs *) {
          LOGF(TESTING, "rom=%d region=%d andy=%d hazel=%d shadow=%d os=%d\n", rom, region, andy, hazel, shadow, os);
          LOGF(TESTING, "suffix=\"%s\"\n", suffix.c_str());
          LOGF(TESTING, "parsed_dso=0x%" PRIx32 "\n", parsed_dso);
      },
                    &num_dsos);

      for (rom = -1; rom < 16; ++rom) {
          uint32_t rom_flags;
          if (!GetOverrideMask(&rom_flags,
                               type, rom < 0 ? -1 : 1,
                               BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_OverrideROM,
                               (uint32_t)rom)) {
              continue;
          }

          static_assert((size_t)(int)NUM_MAPPER_REGIONS == NUM_MAPPER_REGIONS);
          for (region = -1; region < (int)NUM_MAPPER_REGIONS; ++region) {
              uint32_t region_flags;
              if (!GetOverrideMask(&region_flags,
                                   type,
                                   region < 0 ? -1 : 1,
                                   BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_OverrideMapperRegion,
                                   region << BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_MapperRegionShift)) {
                  continue;
              }

              for (andy = -1; andy < 2; ++andy) {
                  uint32_t andy_flags;
                  if (!GetOverrideMask(&andy_flags,
                                       type,
                                       andy,
                                       BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_OverrideANDY,
                                       BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_ANDY)) {
                      continue;
                  }

                  for (hazel = -1; hazel < 2; ++hazel) {
                      uint32_t hazel_flags;
                      if (!GetOverrideMask(&hazel_flags,
                                           type,
                                           hazel,
                                           BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_OverrideHAZEL,
                                           BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_HAZEL)) {
                          continue;
                      }

                      for (shadow = -1; shadow < 2; ++shadow) {
                          uint32_t shadow_flags;
                          if (!GetOverrideMask(&shadow_flags,
                                               type,
                                               shadow,
                                               BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_OverrideShadow,
                                               BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_Shadow)) {
                              continue;
                          }

                          for (os = -1; os < 2; ++os) {
                              uint32_t os_flags;
                              if (!GetOverrideMask(&os_flags,
                                                   type,
                                                   os,
                                                   BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_OverrideOS,
                                                   BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_OS)) {
                                  continue;
                              }

                              uint32_t dso = rom_flags |
                                             region_flags |
                                             andy_flags |
                                             hazel_flags |
                                             shadow_flags |
                                             os_flags;

                              suffix = "?????";

                              suffix = GetSuffixChars(dso);

                              parsed_dso = dso;
                              bool good_parse = ParseAddressSuffix(&parsed_dso, type, suffix.c_str(), nullptr);
                              TEST_TRUE(good_parse);

                              // Invalid test. Irrelevant bits may be set.
                              //TEST_EQ_UU(parsed_dso & ~type->dso_mask, 0);

                              //parsed_dso &= type->dso_mask;
                              //TEST_EQ_UU(parsed_dso, dso);

                              //TEST_EQ_UU(parsed_dso & type->dso_mask, dso & type->dso_mask);

                              //printf("0x%" PRIx32 " : %s (rom=%d region=%d andy=%d hazel=%d shadow=%d os=%d)\n", dso, suffix.c_str(), rom, region, andy, hazel, shadow, os);

                              ++num_dsos;
                          }
                      }
                  }
              }
          }
      }

      RemoveTestFailFnByContext(&num_dsos);

      return num_dsos;
  }

  struct Counts {
      uint32_t b = 0, bplus = 0, master = 0;
  };

  int main() {
      Counts counts_by_type[ROMType_Count];

      for (uint8_t rom_type = 0; rom_type < ROMType_Count; ++rom_type) {
          ROMType rom_types[16];
          for (int i = 0; i < 16; ++i) {
              rom_types[i] = ROMType_16KB;
          }

          // only one needs to be set.
          rom_types[0] = (ROMType)rom_type;

          Counts *c = &counts_by_type[rom_type];

          c->b = TestDSOs(CreateBBCMicroType(BBCMicroTypeID_B, rom_types));
          c->bplus = TestDSOs(CreateBBCMicroType(BBCMicroTypeID_BPlus, rom_types));
          c->master = TestDSOs(CreateBBCMicroType(BBCMicroTypeID_Master, rom_types));
      }

      for (uint8_t rom_type = 0; rom_type < ROMType_Count; ++rom_type) {
          const Counts *c = &counts_by_type[rom_type];
          printf("%s: B: %" PRIu32 "; B+: %" PRIu32 "; Master: %" PRIu32 "\n", GetROMTypeEnumName(rom_type), c->b, c->bplus, c->master);
      }
  }

  #else

  int main() {
      return 0;
  }

  #endif
#+end_src

Convert DSO to suffix string:

#+begin_src c++
  #if BBCMICRO_DEBUGGER
  std::string GetSuffixChars(uint32_t dso) {
      std::string suffix;

      if (dso & BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_Parasite) {
          suffix.push_back(PARASITE_CODE);
      }

      if (dso & BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_OverrideParasiteROM) {
          if (dso & BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_ParasiteROM) {
              suffix.push_back(PARASITE_ROM_CODE);
          }
      }

      if (dso & BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_OverrideROM) {
          suffix.push_back(ROM_BANK_CODES[dso & BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_ROM]);
      } else if ((dso & BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_OverrideANDY | BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_ANDY) == BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_OverrideANDY) {
          // ugly logic but it puts any ROM-related chars in the same place.
          suffix.push_back(ROM_CODE);
      }

      if (dso & BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_OverrideMapperRegion) {
          suffix.push_back(MAPPER_REGION_CODES[dso >> BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_MapperRegionShift & BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_MapperRegionMask]);
      }

      if (dso & BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_OverrideANDY) {
          if (dso & BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_ANDY) {
              suffix.push_back(ANDY_CODE);
          } else {
              // this case was handled above, if required.
          }
      }

      if (dso & BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_OverrideShadow) {
          if (dso & BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_Shadow) {
              suffix.push_back(SHADOW_CODE);
          } else {
              suffix.push_back(MAIN_CODE);
          }
      }

      if (dso & BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_OverrideHAZEL) {
          if (dso & BBCMicroDebugStateOverride_HAZEL) {
              suffix.push_back(HAZEL_CODE);
          } else {
              suffix.push_back(OS_CODE);
          }
      }

      // there's no DSO for the I/O region. See https://github.com/tom-seddon/b2/issues/260

      return suffix;
  }
  #endif
#+end_src

* Byte debug flags spring clean

Need to be able to breakpoint IFJ vs XFJ and ITU vs XTU (and of course
TST).

Add new tables for I/O area. 1 pointer for every 32 bytes, the minimal
resolution (the Tube region being 32 bytes). There's now different
breakpoint-testing code for accessing I/O and ordinary memory in
BBCMicro::UpdateTemplated.

The relevant tables of pointers are held in the corresponding BigPage,
for examination and modification.

The MemoryBigPages has a single such table (since there's only ever
one I/O region), set up by copying the uint8_t ** from the 15th big
page (since that's where the I/O region always is).

#+begin_example
                           C/C++ COMPILER OPTIONS


                                -OPTIMIZATION-

  /O1 maximum optimizations (favor space) /O2 maximum optimizations (favor speed)
  /Ob<n> inline expansion (default n=0)   /Od disable optimizations (default)
  /Og enable global optimization          /Oi[-] enable intrinsic functions
  /Os favor code space                    /Ot favor code speed
  /Ox optimizations (favor speed)         
  /favor:<blend|AMD64|INTEL64|ATOM> select processor to optimize for, one of:
      blend - a combination of optimizations for several different x64 processors
      AMD64 - 64-bit AMD processors
      INTEL64 - Intel(R)64 architecture processors
      ATOM - Intel(R) Atom(TM) processors

                               -CODE GENERATION-

  /Gu[-] ensure distinct functions have distinct addresses
  /Gw[-] separate global variables for linker
  /GF enable read-only string pooling     /Gy[-] separate functions for linker
  /GS[-] enable security checks           /GR[-] enable C++ RTTI
  /guard:cf[-] enable CFG (control flow guard)
  /guard:ehcont[-] enable EH continuation metadata (CET)
  /EHs enable C++ EH (no SEH exceptions)  /EHa enable C++ EH (w/ SEH exceptions)
  /EHc extern "C" defaults to nothrow     
  /EHr always generate noexcept runtime termination checks
  /fp:<contract|except[-]|fast|precise|strict> choose floating-point model:
      contract - consider floating-point contractions when generating code
      except[-] - consider floating-point exceptions when generating code
      fast - "fast" floating-point model; results are less predictable
      precise - "precise" floating-point model; results are predictable
      strict - "strict" floating-point model (implies /fp:except)
  /Qfast_transcendentals generate inline FP intrinsics even with /fp:except
  /Qspectre[-] enable mitigations for CVE 2017-5753
  /Qpar[-] enable parallel code generation
  /Qpar-report:1 auto-parallelizer diagnostic; indicate parallelized loops
  /Qpar-report:2 auto-parallelizer diagnostic; indicate loops not parallelized
  /Qvec-report:1 auto-vectorizer diagnostic; indicate vectorized loops
  /Qvec-report:2 auto-vectorizer diagnostic; indicate loops not vectorized
  /GL[-] enable link-time code generation 
  /volatile:<iso|ms> choose volatile model:
      iso - Acquire/release semantics not guaranteed on volatile accesses
      ms  - Acquire/release semantics guaranteed on volatile accesses
  /GA optimize for Windows Application    /Ge force stack checking for all funcs
  /Gs[num] control stack checking calls   /Gh enable _penter function call
  /GH enable _pexit function call         /GT generate fiber-safe TLS accesses
  /RTC1 Enable fast checks (/RTCsu)       /RTCc Convert to smaller type checks
  /RTCs Stack Frame runtime checking      /RTCu Uninitialized local usage checks
  /clr[:option] compile for common language runtime, where option is:
      pure : produce IL-only output file (no native executable code)
      safe : produce IL-only verifiable output file
      netcore : produce assemblies targeting .NET Core runtime
      noAssembly : do not produce an assembly
      nostdlib : ignore the system .NET framework directory when searching for assemblies
      nostdimport : do not import any required assemblies implicitly
      initialAppDomain : enable initial AppDomain behavior of Visual C++ 2002
      implicitKeepAlive- : turn off implicit emission of System::GC::KeepAlive(this)
      char_t- : turn off metadata support for char8_t, char16_t and char32_t
      ECMAParamArray : use rules specified in ECMA-372/14.6 for overloads with parameter arrays (implied by /clr)
      ECMAParamArray- : use new rules for overloads with parameter arrays (implied by /clr:netcore)
  /fsanitize=address Enable address sanitizer codegen
  /homeparams Force parameters passed in registers to be written to the stack
  /GZ Enable stack checks (/RTCs)         /Gv __vectorcall calling convention
  (Preview) /dynamicdeopt Enable dynamic debugging; place deoptimized breakpoints and step in anywhere with on-demand function deoptimization
  (Preview) /dynamicdeopt:suffix <suffix> File extension suffix for deoptimized output (default: .alt)
  (Preview) /dynamicdeopt:sync Build deoptimized output after optimized output instead of in parallel
  /arch:<SSE2|SSE4.2|AVX|AVX2|AVX512|AVX10.x> minimum CPU architecture requirements, one of:
     SSE2 - (default) enable use of instructions available with SSE2-enabled CPUs
     SSE4.2 - enable use of instructions available with SSE 4.2-enabled CPUs
     AVX - enable use of instructions available with AVX-enabled CPUs
     AVX2 - enable use of instructions available with AVX2-enabled CPUs
     AVX512 - enable use of instructions available with AVX-512-enabled CPUs
     AVX10.x - enable use of instructions available with AVX10.x-enabled CPUs. Valid values of x are 1
  /QIntel-jcc-erratum enable mitigations for Intel JCC erratum
  /Qspectre-load Enable spectre mitigations for all instructions which load memory
  /Qspectre-load-cf Enable spectre mitigations for all control-flow instructions which load memory
  /Qspectre-jmp[-] Enable spectre mitigations for unconditional jump instructions
  /fpcvt:<IA|BC> FP to unsigned integer conversion compatibility
     IA - results compatible with VCVTTSD2USI instruction
     BC - results compatible with VS2017 and earlier compiler
  /jumptablerdata Place jump tables for switch case statements in .rdata section
  /vlen=<256|512> Choose vector length of either 256 or 512 for automatic code-generation
  /vlen Choose default vector length based on /arch setting

                                -OUTPUT FILES-

  /Fa[file] name assembly listing file    /FA[scu] configure assembly listing
  /Fd[file] name .PDB file                /Fe<file> name executable file
  /Fm[file] name map file                 /Fo<file> name object file
  /Fp<file> name precompiled header file  /Fr[file] name source browser file
  /FR[file] name extended .SBR file       /Fi[file] name preprocessed file
  /Fd: <file> name .PDB file              /Fe: <file> name executable file
  /Fm: <file> name map file               /Fo: <file> name object file
  /Fp: <file> name .PCH file              /FR: <file> name extended .SBR file
  /Fi: <file> name preprocessed file      
  /Ft<dir> location of the header files generated for #import
  /doc[file] process XML documentation comments and optionally name the .xdc file

                                -PREPROCESSOR-

  /AI<dir> add to assembly search path    /FU<file> import .NET assembly/module
  /FU:asFriend<file> import .NET assembly/module as friend
  /C don't strip comments                 /D<name>{=|#}<text> define macro
  /E preprocess to stdout                 /EP preprocess to stdout, no #line
  /P preprocess to file                   /Fx merge injected code to file
  /FI<file> name forced include file      /U<name> remove predefined macro
  /u remove all predefined macros         /I<dir> add to include search path
  /X ignore "standard places"             
  /PH generate #pragma file_hash when preprocessing
  /PD print all macro definitions         

                                  -LANGUAGE-

  /std:<c++14|c++17|c++20|c++latest> C++ standard version
      c++14 - ISO/IEC 14882:2014 (default)
      c++17 - ISO/IEC 14882:2017
      c++20 - ISO/IEC 14882:2020
      c++latest - latest draft standard (feature set subject to change)
  /std:<c11|c17|clatest> C standard version
      c11 - ISO/IEC 9899:2011
      c17 - ISO/IEC 9899:2018
      clatest - latest draft standard (feature set subject to change)
  /permissive[-] enable some nonconforming code to compile
                 (feature set subject to change) (off by default in C++20 and later)
  /Za disable extensions (not recommended for C++)
  /ZW enable WinRT language extensions    /Zs syntax check only
  /await enable resumable functions extension
  /await:strict enable standard C++20 coroutine support with earlier language versions
  /constexpr:depth<N>     recursion depth limit for constexpr evaluation (default: 512)
  /constexpr:backtrace<N> show N constexpr evaluations in diagnostics (default: 10)
  /constexpr:steps<N>     terminate constexpr evaluation after N steps (default: 1048576)
  /Zi enable debugging information        /Z7 enable old-style debug info
  /Zo[-] generate richer debugging information for optimized code (on by default)
  /ZH:[MD5|SHA1|SHA_256] hash algorithm for calculation of file checksum in debug info (default: SHA_256)
  /Zp[n] pack structs on n-byte boundary  /Zl omit default library name in .OBJ
  /vd{0|1|2} disable/enable vtordisp      /vm<x> type of pointers to members
  /Zc:arg1[,arg2] language conformance, where arguments can be:
    forScope[-]           enforce Standard C++ for scoping rules
    wchar_t[-]            wchar_t is the native type, not a typedef
    auto[-]               enforce the new Standard C++ meaning for auto
    trigraphs[-]          enable trigraphs (off by default)
    rvalueCast[-]         enforce Standard C++ explicit type conversion rules
                          (on by default in C++20 or later, implied by /permissive-)
    strictStrings[-]      disable string-literal to [char|wchar_t]*
                          conversion (on by default in C++20 or later, implied by /permissive-)
    implicitNoexcept[-]   enable implicit noexcept on required functions
    threadSafeInit[-]     enable thread-safe local static initialization
    inline[-]             remove unreferenced function or data if it is
                          COMDAT or has internal linkage only (off by default)
    sizedDealloc[-]       enable C++14 global sized deallocation
                          functions (on by default)
    throwingNew[-]        assume operator new throws on failure (off by default)
    referenceBinding[-]   a temporary will not bind to a non-const
                          lvalue reference (on by default in C++20 or later, implied by /permissive-)
    twoPhase-             disable two-phase name lookup
    ternary[-]            enforce C++11 rules for conditional operator
                          (on by default in C++20 or later, implied by /permissive-)
    noexceptTypes[-]      enforce C++17 noexcept rules (on by default in C++17 or later)
    alignedNew[-]         enable C++17 alignment of dynamically allocated objects (on by default)
    hiddenFriend[-]       enforce Standard C++ hidden friend rules
                          (on by default in C++20 or later, implied by /permissive-)
    externC[-]            enforce Standard C++ rules for 'extern "C"' functions
                          (on by default in C++20 or later, implied by /permissive-)
    lambda[-]             better lambda support by using the newer lambda processor
                          (on by default in C++20 or later, implied by /permissive-)
    tlsGuards[-]          generate runtime checks for TLS variable initialization (on by default)
    zeroSizeArrayNew[-]   call member new/delete for 0-size arrays of objects (on by default)
    static_assert[-]      strict handling of 'static_assert' (on by default in C++20 or later,
                          implied by /permissive-)
    gotoScope[-]          cannot jump past the initialization of a variable (implied by /permissive-)
    templateScope[-]      enforce Standard C++ template parameter shadowing rules
    enumTypes[-]          enable Standard C++ underlying enum types (off by default)
    checkGwOdr[-]         enforce Standard C++ one definition rule violations
                          when /Gw has been enabled (off by default)
    nrvo[-]               enable optional copy and move elision (on by default in C++20 or later,
                          implied by /permissive- or /O2)
    __STDC__              define __STDC__ to 1 in C
    __cplusplus[-]        __cplusplus macro reports the supported C++ standard (off by default)
    char8_t[-]            enable C++20 native `u8` literal support as `const char8_t`
                          (on by default in C++20 or later)
    externConstexpr[-]    enable external linkage for constexpr variables in C++
                          (on by default in C++20 or later, implied by /permissive-)
    preprocessor[-]       enable standard conforming preprocessor in C/C++
                          (on by default in C11 or later)
  /ZI enable Edit and Continue debug info 
  /openmp enable OpenMP 2.0 language extensions
  /openmp:experimental enable OpenMP 2.0 language extensions plus select OpenMP 3.0+ language extensions
  /openmp:llvm OpenMP language extensions using LLVM runtime

                                -MISCELLANEOUS-

  @<file> options response file           /?, /help print this help message
  /bigobj generate extended object format /c compile only, no link
  /FC use full pathnames in diagnostics   /H<num> max external name length
  /J default char type is unsigned

  /nologo suppress copyright message      /showIncludes show include file names
  /Tc<source file> compile file as .c     /Tp<source file> compile file as .cpp
  /TC compile all files as .c             /TP compile all files as .cpp
  /V<string> set version string           /Yc[file] create .PCH file

  /execution-charset:<iana-name>|.nnnn set execution character set
  /utf-8 set source and execution character set to UTF-8

  /fastfail[-] enable fast-fail mode      /JMC[-] enable native just my code
  /presetPadding[-] zero initialize padding for stack based class types
  /volatileMetadata[-] generate metadata on volatile memory accesses
  /sourcelink [file] file containing source link information

                                  -LINKING-

  /LD Create .DLL                         /LDd Create .DLL debug library
  /LN Create a .netmodule                 /F<num> set stack size
  /link [linker options and libraries]    /MD link with MSVCRT.LIB
  /MT link with LIBCMT.LIB                /MDd link with MSVCRTD.LIB debug lib
  /MTd link with LIBCMTD.LIB debug lib    

                                -CODE ANALYSIS-

  /analyze[-] Enable native analysis      /analyze:quiet[-] No warning to console
  /analyze:log<name> Warnings to file     /analyze:autolog Log to *.pftlog
  /analyze:autolog:ext<ext> Log to *.<ext>/analyze:autolog- No log file
  /analyze:WX- Warnings not fatal         /analyze:stacksize<num> Max stack frame
  /analyze:max_paths<num> Max paths       /analyze:only Analyze, no code gen

                                -DIAGNOSTICS-

  /diagnostics:<args,...> controls the format of diagnostic messages:
               classic   - retains prior format
               column[-] - prints column information
               caret[-]  - prints column and the indicated line of source
  /Wall enable all warnings               /w   disable all warnings
  /W<n> set warning level (default n=1)   
  /Wv:xx[.yy[.zzzzz]] disable warnings introduced after version xx.yy.zzzzz
  /WX treat warnings as errors            /WL enable one line diagnostics
  /wd<n> disable warning n                /we<n> treat warning n as an error
  /wo<n> issue warning n once             /w<l><n> set warning level 1-4 for n
  /external:I <path>      - location of external headers
  /external:env:<var>     - environment variable with locations of external headers
  /external:anglebrackets - treat all headers included via <> as external
  /external:W<n>          - warning level for external headers
  /external:templates[-]  - evaluate warning level across template instantiation chain
  /sdl enable additional security features and warnings
  /options:strict unrecognized compiler options are an error
#+end_example

* MMFS stuff

#+begin_src markdown
  ## Brief architecture

  MMFS is implemented as additional hardware in b2, similar to SCSI:

  - **MMFS Class** (`src/beeb/src/MMFS.cpp`) - Emulates MMC/SD card via
    SPI protocol
  - **Memory-mapped I/O** - Registers at different addresses depending
    on machine type:
    - BBC B: `0xFE1C`
    - Master: `0xFEDC`
    - (The ROM has these addresses compiled in; the emulator
      auto-detects the machine type)
  - **Configuration** (`src/b2/MMFSConfig.h`) - Stores image path and
    debug settings
  - **UI Integration** (`src/b2/ConfigsUI.cpp`) - Checkbox and file
    browser

  ### SPI Protocol

  The implementation handles standard MMC commands:

  - **CMD0**: Reset card (returns `0x01`)
  - **CMD1**: Initialize card (must be called twice: first returns
    `0x01`, second returns `0x00`)
  - **CMD10**: Read card ID (returns 20-byte identification)
  - **CMD16**: Set block length (typically 512 bytes)
  - **CMD17**: Read data block (512-byte sector)
  - **CMD24**: Write data block (512-byte sector)

  ### Initialization flow

  ```
  User enables MMFS in UI
      ↓
  Config saved with mmfs_enabled=true and image_path
      ↓
  BeebThread creates BBCMicro with BBCMicroInitFlag_MMFS
      ↓
  BBCMicroState creates MMFS instance
      ↓
  BBCMicro detects machine type and registers MMIO handlers
    (0xFE1C for BBC B, 0xFEDC for Master)
      ↓
  MMFS ROM communicates with emulated card
  ```

  The emulation is ROM-agnostic and works with both MMFS v1 and v2 ROMs
  by providing block-level access to the image file. The correct
  memory-mapped I/O address is automatically selected based on the
  emulated machine type to match the address compiled into the
  corresponding ROM.
#+end_src

* Symbols/DSO stuff

The current mechanism may need a bit of tweaking.

** Inclusive/exclusive

(TODO: naming)

Each symbol in a file only has an address attached, and the BBC uses
paging a lot. How should the debugger decide when a symbol actually
applies?

There's 2 ways of interpreting the label file's suffixes.

- Exclusive :: if the region corresponding to one of the suffixes is
  visible, symbols in the file apply; otherwise, symbols in the file
  are hidden. For example, suppose =labels.sym= is loaded and its
  suffixes are =f;e= - then if ROM bank f or e is paged in, all
  symbols from =labels.sym= are shown. Otherwise - i.e., ROM banks
  0-9, a-d - all of its symbols are hidden.
  
  This makes sense for paged ROMs. Symbols inside the ROM area are
  only relevant if the ROM is paged in - and that probably applies to
  symbols outside the ROM area too. They'll be ROM workspace and so
  on, not relevant when some other ROM is paged in.

- Inclusive :: if the region corresponding to one of the suffixes is
  visible effect, symbols in the file apply if their address is
  covered by that region. Symbols with addresses covered by other
  regions are always visible.

  This is probably what you want for general code that might cross
  regions. 

** Actual bytes are ambiguous

e.g., if a file's suffixes are =f;e=, and symbol =x= is $8000, then
the address of =x= is easy enough, but which byte is it?

** Exclusive symbols are debugger only

For example, suppose =labels.sym= has suffix =f= and =x\=0= and
exclusive. Now =x= will only be shown in the debugger when bank f is
paged in. But if you put a byte breakpoint on =x=, it'll have to end
up at =$0000`m=! The ROM bank will never play a part.

This is probably manageable from the debugger. You need to right click
on the byte and it shows you the byte address there.

Feels like the UX for this might not be ideal if using the HTTP API
though.

** More TODOs

- with the ROM mappers, the same byte can be visible at multiple
  addresses. Are the debug flags set up to handle this?

* snapcraft build stuff

Get snap/lxc/etc. set up on Linux PC, by whatever means necessary.

One-time setup:

: git clone git@github.com:popey/b2-snap

Iteration, from inside the =b2-snap= working copy:

: snapcraft clean
: snapcraft pack --use-lxd --verbose --debug

- =--use-lxd= :: as per popey's suggestion
- =--verbose= :: reproduce container stdout/stderr during the build
- =--debug= :: pop you into a shell in the container when the build
  fails

* Headless mode

Activate using =--headless=.

- Implies =--imgui-enable-test-engine=

- Doesn't initialise sound or graphics, creates no window, and times
  itself via a timer rather than vsync. The intention is that it be
  operated via the HTTP API or Dear ImGui Test Engine

- Requires =--config-folder=, to avoid polluting the normal config
  files

- Opens the HTTP server on a random port. Writes a file called
  =b2_http_listen_port.txt= to the config folder, a text file
  containing the port number in decimal

Sample command line:

#+begin_example
  --headless --imgui-enable-test-engine  --config-folder=D:/temp/b2_tests.quit --imgui-run-test=b2_tests.quit
#+end_example

#+begin_quote
Note: The real ULA uses line 281->283/4 for VSYNC, but on both my TVs
this loses part of the top line. So here we move the screen down by 7
rows. This should be transparent to software, as it doesn't affect the
timing of the display or RTC interrupts. I'm happy to rever this is
anyone complains!
#+end_quote

*** TODO Some command key stuff

The Electron doesn't have function keys. Very convenient for the
debugger keys!

Check that command keys that aren't mapped to a BBC key are always
doing the command. Hopefully already the case.

Update code so if a command key is mapped to a BBC key, but that BBC
key has no corresponding key in the emulated system, it's treated as
if unmapped. So that even when prioritize command keys is unticked on
Electron, you can still use the default debugger keys.

Keycap keymaps (once they actually work when emulating Electron...)
will interfere with this, but that's as expected..

   1230REM  &FC72 Electron expansion status
   1240REM         Read: b7=BUSY, b6=ADC, b5=Fire1, b4=Fire2
   1250REM  &FC73 Electron
   1260REM         Write: ROMSTROBE
   1270REM
   1280REM &FCC0-08 Floppy disk controller
   1290REM  &FCC0 Drive select register
   1300REM  &FCC4 FDC command/status
   1310REM  &FCC7 FDC data
   1320REM
   1330REM &FCD8/&FCD9 lock/unlock register bank 5/6
   1340REM &FCDA/&FCDB lock/unlock register bank 13/15
   1350REM &FCDC/&FCDD lock/unlock register bank 0/2
   1360REM &FCDE/&FCDF lock/unlock register bank 1/3
   1370REM
   1380REM &FCFC AQR paging register
   1390REM &FCFD unlock AQR
   1400REM &FCFE lock AQR
   1410:
   1420:
   1430file$=name$+"v"+LEFT$(ver$,1)+MID$(ver$,3,2)
   1440FOR P=0 TO 1:opt%=P*2+4
   1450P%=load%:O%=mcode%
   1460[OPT opt%
   1470.exec%
   1480.L8000:JMP L8021                         :\ Language entry
   1490.L8003:JMP PlusOne                       :\ Service entry
   1500.L8006:EQUB &C0                          :\ ROM type=Serv+Lang+6502 BASIC
   1510.L8007:EQUB L8019-L8000-1                :\ Copyright offset
   1520.L8008:EQUB EVAL("&"+MID$(verROM$,3,2))  :\ ROM version byte
   1530.L8009
   1540OPT FNif(TARGET%<>0)
   1550EQUS MID$("6502EmBBC B+MasterCompact",((TARGET%+1)AND-2)*3+1,6+3*(TARGET%=1)-(TARGET%=5))+" "
   1560OPT FNendif
   1570\EQUS "RH Plus 1/AP6":\EQUB 0            :\ ROM title
   1580EQUS "RH Plus 1":EQUB 0                  :\ ROM title
   1590EQUS LEFT$(verROM$,4)+LEFT$(CHR$(96+VALRIGHT$(verROM$,1)),LENverROM$-4) :\ Version string
   1600EQUS " ("+dateROM$+")":EQUB 0            :\ Version date
   1610.L8019
   1620EQUS "(C)J.G.Harston":EQUB 0             :\ Copyright string
   1630.NULL
   1640RTS
   1660.PlusOne
   1670JSR NULL:JMP L8067
   1680EQUB &82:EQUB PlusCopy-PlusOne-1:EQUB EVAL("&"+MID$(ver$,3,2))
   1690EQUS "Plus 1 Support":EQUB 0
   1700EQUS LEFT$(ver$,4)+" ("+date$+")":EQUB 0 :\ Version string
   1710.PlusCopy
   1720EQUS "(C)JGH & Acorn":EQUB 0
   1750\ LANGUAGE HANDLER
   1760\ ================
   1770\ Languages higher than me are disabled, so I am entered by default.
   1780\ Restore ROM table then try to enter default language. If no default look
   1790\ for a default language lower than me, then above me.
   1800\ Results in my ROM name being displayed then new default ROM name displayed.
   1810\
   1820.L8021
   1830LDA #&A3:LDX #&80        :\ OSBYTE &A3,&80,&04
   1840LDY #&04:JSR OSBYTE      :\ Restore ROM table, find default language
   1850.L803B
   1860TXA:BMI Prompt           :\ No language, or entering me as a language
   1870LDA #142:JMP OSBYTE      :\ Enter as a language
   1880:
   1890\ No-Language Environment
   1900\ -----------------------
   1910.Report
   1920JSR OSNEWL:LDY #1
   1930.ReportLp
   1940LDA (&FD),Y:BEQ ReportDone
   1950JSR OSWRCH:INY:BNE ReportLp
   1960.ReportDone
   1970JSR OSNEWL
   1980.Prompt
   1990LDX #&FF:TXS:CLI
   2000JSR ClaimBRKV
   2010LDA #ASC"*":JSR OSWRCH
   2020LDX #Osword0 AND 255
   2030LDY #Osword0 DIV 256
   2040LDA #0:JSR OSWORD:BCS PromptEsc
   2050LDX #0:LDY #7:JSR OS_CLI
   2060JMP Prompt
   2070.PromptEsc
   2080LDA #126:JSR OSBYTE
   2090BRK:EQUB 17:EQUS "Escape":BRK
   2100.Osword0
   2110EQUW &0700:EQUB 255:EQUB 32:EQUB 255
   2130.Serv10
   2140LDA &203:CMP #&C0
   2150PLA:BCC ClaimBRKexit   :\ Only claim if MOS has BRKV
   2160.ClaimBRKV
   2170LDX #&03:STX &202
   2180LDX #&FF:STX &203
   2190LDX #Report AND 255:STX &DA2
   2200LDX #Report DIV 256:STX &DA3
   2210LDX &F4:STX &DA4
   2220.ClaimBRKexit
   2230RTS
   2260\ SERVICE HANDLER
   2270\ ===============
   2280.L8067
   2290PHA
   2300LDA &0DF0,X:BMI L80CD:\ If b7=1, disabled, ignore service call
   2330\ Dispatch service call
   2340\ ---------------------
   2350.L808E
   2360PLA:PHA:TAX          :\ Get service call back
   2370CPX #&16:BCS L80CC   :\ Unsupported, exit
   2380ASL A:TAX            :\ Index into dispatch table
   2390LDA L80A0+1,X:PHA    :\ Stack high byte of address
   2400LDA L80A0+0,X:PHA    :\ Stack low byte of address
   2410RTS                  :\ Return to jump to routine
   2430\ Service call dispatch table
   2440\ ---------------------------
   2450.L80A0
   2460EQUW L80CC-1 :\ &00 - Null
   2470EQUW L815E-1 :\ &01 - Private workspace
   2480EQUW L80CC-1 :\ &02 - Null
   2490EQUW L80CC-1 :\ &03 - Null
   2500EQUW L80CC-1 :\ &04 - Null
   2510EQUW L81A6-1 :\ &05 - Interupt
   2520EQUW L8617-1 :\ &06 - Error
   2530EQUW L83BA-1 :\ &07 - OSBYTE
   2540EQUW L80CC-1 :\ &08 - Null
   2550EQUW L80CC-1 :\ &09 - Null
   2560EQUW L80CC-1 :\ &0A - Null
   2570EQUW L80CC-1 :\ &0B - Null
   2580EQUW L80CC-1 :\ &0C - Null
   2590EQUW L80CC-1 :\ &0D - Null
   2600EQUW L80CC-1 :\ &0E - Null
   2610EQUW L8690-1 :\ &0F - Vectors changed
   2620EQUW Serv10-1:\ &10 - Close Spool/Exec
   2630EQUW L80CC-1 :\ &11 - Null
   2640EQUW L80CC-1 :\ &12 - Null
   2650EQUW L8258-1 :\ &13 - Character in serial buffer
   2660EQUW L8261-1 :\ &14 - Character in printer buffer
   2670EQUW L8232-1 :\ &15 - 100Hz poll
   2690.L80CC:LDX &F4          :\ Restore registers and return
   2700.L80CD:PLA:RTS          :\ Restore A and return
   2710.L80D0:STA &F0          :\ Pass A to returned X
   2720.L80D1:LDX &F4          :\ Restore ROM to X
   2730PLA:LDA #&00:RTS        :\ Claim call and return
   2760\ SERVICE 1 - Shared workspace
   2770\ ============================
   2780\ Set up Plus 1 support
   2790.L815E
   2800TYA:PHA                    :\ Save workspace pointer
   2810OPT FNif(TARGET%<1)
   2820  LDA &FFB2
   2830  CMP #&40:BEQ L8160       :\ Running on Electron?
   2840  LDX &F4:LDA &0DF0,X
   2850  ORA #&80:STA &0DF0,X     :\ Not Electron, disable myself
   2860  .L8160
   2870OPT FNendif
   2880LDA #0:STA &0D68           :\ No 100Hz calls enabled, ROM table not moved, nothing disabled
   2890STA &0D6B:STA &FC73        :\ Set ROMSTROBE and copy to &00
   2900OPT FNif(TARGET%<1)
   2910  LDA &28D                 :\ Check Break type
   2920OPT FNendif
   2930BEQ L8182                  :\ Soft Break (or not Electron), don't move ROM table
   2940LDX &F4:BPL L816E          :\ Jump to start at the ROM above me
   2950.L8163
   2960LDA ROMtable,X:STA &0380,X :\ Copy ROM table to &0380-&038F
   2970AND #&BF:STA ROMtable,X    :\ Remove language bit from ROM byte
   2980.L816E
   2990INX:CPX #&10:BCC L8163
   3000LDA #4:STA &0D68           :\ Set 'ROM table moved'
   3010.L8182
   3020LDX #&04:JSR L8388         :\ Set maximum ADC channel to 4
   3030LDA &028D:CLC:BEQ L8192    :\ Soft Break, don't set defaults
   3040.L818C
   3050OPT FNif(FALSE)
   3060  LDA &28F:AND #&CF:STA &28F :\ Reset disk access speed
   3070OPT FNendif
   3080LDX #&01:STX &285          :\ Set Printer Type to 1 (parallel)
   3090SEC                        :\ SEC=use default serial speed
   3100.L8192
   3110JSR L8567                  :\ Initialise serial interface
   3120PLA:TAY:JMP L80CC
   3150\ SERVICE 5 - Interrupt
   3160\ =====================
   3170.L81A6
   3180LDA #&02
   3190BIT &0D68:BNE L81BB     :\ If IRQ Ignore, exit
   3200LDA &0D6A:BPL L81BB     :\ Serial Mask b7=0, ignore all IRQs
   3210AND &FC65:BMI L81BE     :\ Mask with 6281 IRQ, b7=input port changed (RTS)
   3220LSR A:BCS L81CB         :\ b0 set -> TxRDY
   3230LSR A:BCS L8206         :\ b1 set -> RxRDY
   3240.L81BB
   3250JMP L80CC               :\ Restore all and return unclaimed
   3270.L81BE
   3280LDA #&04                :\ Test bit 2
   3290BIT &FC64:BNE L81C8     :\ Input bit 2 not changes
   3300JSR L85AA               :\
   3310.L81C8
   3320JMP L80D1               :\ Claim call and return
   3340.L81CB
   3350LDA #&04                :\ Test CTS in bit 2
   3360BIT &FC6D:BNE L8200     :\ Not CTS, skip to set serial inactive
   3370JSR L85CD               :\ Get byte from SerOut buffer
   3380BCC L81EA               :\ If not empty, write it to SerTx
   3390LDX &0285               :\ Get Printer Type
   3400CPX #&02:BNE L81F2      :\ Not 2=Serial
   3410JSR L85D3               :\ Get byte from Printer Buffer
   3420PHA:PHP
   3430JSR L87AB               :\ Set/Clear printer Buffer Busy flag
   3440PLP:PLA
   3450BCS L81F2               :\ Printer Buffer was empty, skip past
   3460.L81EA
   3470STA &FC63               :\ Write byte to DataTx
   3480LDX #&00:JMP L81FA      :\ Set SerialFlag to 0 and return
   3500.L81F2
   3510JSR L85B9           :\ Set serial inactive
   3520JSR L82EB           :\ Set/Clear CTS from buffer space
   3530LDX #&FF            :\ Set SerialFlag to &FF and return
   3540.L81FA
   3550STX &024F           :\ Serial Use Flag
   3560JMP L80D1           :\ Claim call and return
   3580.L8200
   3590JSR L85B9           :\ Set serial inactive
   3600JMP L80D1           :\ Claim call and return
   3620.L8206
   3630LDX &FC63           :\ Read from 2681 Data Register
   3640LDA &FC61           :\ Read from 2681 Status Register
   3650AND #&F0:BNE L8221  :\ If any error bit set, skip to generate event
   3660TXA:JSR L85C8       :\ Insert into serial input buffer
   3670JSR L85D9:BCS L821E :\ At least 8 bytes left, exit
   3680LDA #&01:STA &FC6F  :\ Raise RTS to stop other end sending
   3690.L821E
   3700JMP L80D1           :\ Claim and return
   3720.L8221
   3730TXA:PHA             :\ Save byte read
   3740LDX &FC61           :\ Read from 2681 Status again
   3750JSR L85A0           :\ Reset 6281 Error Status
   3760PLA                 :\ Get byte back
   3770LDY #&07:JSR &FFBF  :\ Generate Event 7 - Serial Error
   3780JMP L80D1           :\ Claim and return
   3810\ SERVICE &15 - 100Hz Poll
   3820\ ========================
   3830.L8232
   3840DEY:TYA:PHA         :\ Decrement semaphore
   3850LDA #&40:BIT &0D68  :\ Check printer buffer active
   3860BEQ L823F:JSR L827D :\ If active, try to send character to printer
   3870.L823F
   3880LDA #&20:BIT &0D68  :\ Check if ADC active
   3890BEQ L8249:JSR L82A1 :\ If active, check for ADC conversion
   3900.L8249
   3910LDA #&10:BIT &0D68  :\ Check if serial active
   3920BEQ L8253:JSR L82EB
   3930.L8253
   3940PLA:TAY             :\ Restore semaphore
   3950JMP L80CC           :\ Return unclaimed
   3970\ Character entering serial output buffer
   3980\ =======================================
   3990.L8258
   4000JSR L85AA           :\ Write something to serial port
   4010JSR L82F2           :\ Set/Clear CTS if input buffer almost full
   4020JMP L80D1           :\ Claim call and return
   4040\ Character entering printer output buffer
   4050\ ========================================
   4060.L8261
   4070TYA:PHA             :\ Preserve Y
   4080JSR L826B           :\ Process output character
   4090PLA:TAY:JMP L80D1   :\ Restore, claim call and return
   4110.L826B
   4120LDX &0285           :\ Get printer destination
   4130CPX #&01:BEQ L827D  :\ Parallel printer
   4140CPX #&02:BNE L827C  :\ Not serial printer
   4160\ Output to serial printer
   4170\ ------------------------
   4180JSR L85AA           :\ Write something to serial port
   4190JSR L82F2           :\ Set/Clear CTS if input buffer almost full
   4200.L827C
   4210RTS
   4230\ Output to parallel printer
   4240\ --------------------------
   4250.L827D
   4260BIT &FC72:BMI L829C :\ If printer busy, turn 100Hz poll on and return
   4270JSR L85D3           :\ Get character from printer buffer
   4280BCS L8293           :\ If buffer empty, turn off 100Hz poll
   4290STA &FC71           :\ Send to printer port
   4300LDA #&40:JSR L85EA  :\ Set b6 of &0D68, turn on 100Hz poll
   4310CLC:JMP L8299       :\ Clear Printer Buffer Busy flag and return
   4330\ Printer buffer empty
   4340\ --------------------
   4350.L8293
   4360LDA #&40:JSR L85FD  :\ Clear b6 of &0D68, turn off 100Hz poll
   4370SEC                 :\ Set b7 of &02C6 and return
   4380                    :\ Set Serial Error event flag
   4390.L8299
   4400JMP L87AB           :\ Set/Clear Printer Buffer Busy flag
   4420\ Printer busy
   4430\ ------------
   4440.L829C
   4450LDA #&40:JMP L85EA  :\ Set b6 of &0D68, turn on 100Hz poll
   4480\ Check for ADC conversion
   4490\ ========================
   4500.L82A1
   4510BIT &FC72:BVS L82FC   :\ If no ADC ready, return
   4520LDX &024C             :\ Get current ADC channel
   4530TXA:BEQ L82FC         :\ No ADC channel, return
   4540LDA #&00:STA &02F7,X  :\ Set low byte to zero
   4550LDA &FC70:STA &02FB,X :\ Set high byte from ADC
   4560STX &02F7             :\ Store as last channel converted
   4570LDY #&03:JSR &FFBF    :\ Event 3 - ADC complete
   4580LDX &02F7             :\ Get last channel converted
   4590DEX                   :\ Step down to next channel
   4600BNE L82D0             :\ If nonzero, start this channel
   4610LDX &024D             :\ Get maximum ADC channel
   4620TXA:BNE L82D0         :\ If nonzero, start this channel
   4630LDA #&20:JMP L85FD    :\ Clear b5 of &0D68, turn off 100Hz poll
   4640.L82D0
   4650.L82D3
   4660CPX #&05:BCC L82D9    :\ If channel<5, use it
   4670LDX #&04              :\ Channel>4, force to channel 4
   4680.L82D9
   4690TXA:STX &024C         :\ Store as current channel
   4700TAX:BEQ L82E6         :\ If channel zero, exit
   4710LDA L82E7-1,X         :\ Get control value for this channel
   4720STA &FC70             :\ Write to ADC control
   4730.L82E6
   4740RTS
   4760\ ADC control values
   4770\ ------------------
   4780.L82E7
   4790EQUB &04:EQUB &05
   4800EQUB &06:EQUB &07
   4820\ Set CTS according to input buffer state
   4830\ ---------------------------------------
   4840.L82EB
   4850LDA &0D6A
   4860AND #&02:BEQ L82F7  :\ Serial input buffer inactive, raise CTS
   4880\ Set/Clear CTS if serial input buffer nearly full
   4890\ ------------------------------------------------
   4900.L82F2
   4910JSR L85D9           :\ Count space in serial input buffer
   4920BCS L82FD           :\ Still space left in input buffer
   4930.L82F7
   4940LDA #&01:STA &FC6F  :\ Raise RTS to pause sender
   4950.L82FC
   4960RTS
   4980.L82FD
   4990LDA #&01:STA &FC6E  :\ Lower RTS to unpause sender
   5000RTS
   5020\ OSBYTE &A3 - Application call
   5030\ =============================
   5040.L8303
   5050LDA &F0
   5060CMP #&80:BNE L8319     :\ Not reason &80, exit
   5070LDX &F1:CPX #4
   5080BEQ L831C              :\ Subcall &04 - restore ROM table
   5090BCS L8319              :\ Subcall >&04 - exit
   5100TXA:ROR A              :\ Cy=b0 of subcall, enable/disable flag
   5110LDA &0D68:PHA          :\ Save old setting
   5120BCC AppEnable          :\ b0=0, enable setting
   5130ORA AppMask,X          :\ Set a bit in flags
   5140BCS AppReturn
   5150.AppEnable
   5160AND AppMask,X:INX      :\ Clear bit in flags, point to set bit
   5170.AppReturn
   5180STA &0D68              :\ Store new setting
   5190PLA:AND AppMask,X:PHA  :\ Get old setting
   5200TXA:EOR #1:BNE AppDone :\ Subcall 2,3 just return
   5210BCS AppDisable         :\ Subcall 1, disable Plus 1 functions
   5220JSR L8671              :\ Enable Plus 1 functions
   5230JMP AppDone
   5240.AppDisable
   5250JSR L8641              :\ Disable Plus 1 functions
   5260.AppDone
   5270PLA:JMP L80D0          :\ Jump to return old flags
   5280.AppMask
   5290EQUB &FE:EQUB &01
   5300EQUB &FD:EQUB &02
   5310.L8319
   5320JMP L80CC
   5340\ OSBYTE &A3,&80,4 - Restore ROM table
   5350\ ------------------------------------
   5360.L831C
   5370LDA &0D68                  :\ Check system flags
   5380AND #4:BEQ L832A           :\ ROM table not moved
   5390LDX &F4:BPL L8325          :\ Start at the ROM above me
   5400.L831F
   5410LDA &0380,X:STA ROMtable,X :\ Copy &0380-&038F back to ROM table
   5420.L8325
   5430INX:CPX #&10:BCC L831F
   5440LDA &0D68:AND #&FB:STA &0D68 :\ Set 'ROM table not moved'
   5450.L832A
   5460LDY #1                       :\ On first pass, start at Plus 1 ROM
   5470LDA L0D6D:AND #&8F           :\ Get default LANG ROM number
   5480TAX:BPL TryThisROM           :\ b7=0, default ROM
   5490.LookForROMlp
   5500DEY:BNE P%+4:LDX &F4         :\ Start at Plus 1 ROM
   5510DEX:BPL P%+4:LDX #15         :\ Step down, looping around
   5520CPX &F4:BEQ NoLanguage       :\ If looped back to Plus 1, no more left
   5530.TryThisROM
   5540LDA ROMtable,X               :\ Get ROM byte
   5550ASL A:BPL LookForROMlp       :\ No language, try next one down
   5560.NoLanguage
   5570TXA
   5580CMP &F4:BNE P%+4:ORA #&80    :\ If no language found, return &80+Plus1
   5590JMP L80D0                    :\ Jump to return A as returned X
   5620\ OSBYTE &80 - ADVAL
   5630\ ==================
   5640\ ADVAL 1-4 are performed by MOS
   5650.L8366
   5660LDA &F0:BNE L8319   :\ Not ADVAL(0), exit
   5670LDA &FC72:PHA       :\ Read Plus 1 status
   5680BPL L8373:JSR L826B :\ If printer not busy, try to send a character
   5690.L8373
   5700PLA:LSR A:LSR A     :\ Move fire buttons into b0-b1
   5710LSR A:LSR A
   5720AND #&03:EOR #&03   :\ Keep and toggle b0-b1
   5730JMP L80D0           :\ Return fire buttons
   5760\ OSBYTE &10 - Maximum ADC channel
   5770\ ================================
   5780.L837F
   5790LDX &F0:JSR L8388   :\ Set maximum ADC channel
   5800JMP L80D0           :\ Return maximum actually set
   5810.L8388
   5820TXA:BEQ L8395       :\ Zero channels, set and return
   5830JSR L82D3:TXA       :\ Check within range and initialise ADC
   5840PHA
   5850LDA #&20:JSR L85EA  :\ Enable 100Hz poll for ADC
   5860PLA
   5870.L8395
   5880STA &024D:RTS       :\ Store maximum ADC channel
   5900\ OSBYTE &11 - Start ADC conversion
   5910\ =================================
   5920.L839A
   5930LDA #&00:STA &02F7     :\ Clear 'last channel'
   5940LDX &F0:JSR L82D3:TXA  :\ Start conversion on channel X
   5950PHA:LDA #&20:JSR L85EA :\ Enable 100Hz poll for ADC
   5960PLA:JMP L80D0          :\ Return X=channel actually started
   5990\ OSBYTE &6E - Write to ROMSTROBE
   6000\ ===============================
   6010\ Writes X to ROMSTROBE at &FC73 and RAM copy at &0D6B
   6020\ Returns old ROMSTROBE from RAM copy
   6030\
   6040.L83AC
   6050LDX &F0             :\ Get new ROMSTROBE from X parameter
   6060LDA &0D6B           :\ Get old ROMSTROBE
   6070STX &0D6B:STX &FC73 :\ Store new ROMSTROBE and output it
   6080JMP L80D0           :\ Return old ROMSTROBE as X
   6110\ SERVICE 7 - OSBYTE
   6120\ ==================
   6130.L83BA
   6140LDA &EF             :\ Get OSBYTE number
   6150CMP #&80:BEQ L8366  :\  &80   - ADVAL
   6160CMP #&10:BEQ L837F  :\ *FX16  - Maximum ADC channel
   6170CMP #&11:BEQ L839A  :\ *FX17  - Start ADC conversion
   6180CMP #&07:BEQ L83E6  :\ *FX7   - serial receive speed
   6190CMP #&08:BEQ L83EC  :\ *FX8   - serial transmit speed
   6200CMP #&9C:BEQ L8436  :\  &9C   - serial control
   6210CMP #&02:BEQ L840F  :\ *FX2   - input stream
   6220CMP #&6E:BEQ L83AC  :\ *FX110 - write to ROMSTROBE
   6230CMP #&A3:BNE L83E3
   6240JMP L8303           :\  &A3   - application call
   6250.L83E3
   6260JMP L80CC
   6280\ OSBYTE 7 and 8 - set serial speed
   6290\ =================================
   6300\ On entry, X=baud rate setting (0=default)
   6310\ On exit   X=control register setting that wasn't changed
   6330\ OSBYTE &07 - Serial receive speed
   6340\ ----------------------------------
   6350.L83E6
   6360LDA #&F0:STA &F1    :\ Set &F1 to &F0 for 'receive'
   6370BNE L83F0
   6390\ OSBYTE &08 - Serial transmit speed
   6400\ ----------------------------------
   6410.L83EC
   6420LDA #&0F:STA &F1    :\ Set &F1 to &0F for 'transmit'
   6440\ Set serial speed, &F1=control mask
   6450\ ----------------------------------
   6460.L83F0
   6470LDX &F0             :\ Get OSBYTE X value
   6480CPX #&0C:BCS L840C  :\ If speed>11, exit unchanged
   6490LDA &F1:EOR #&FF    :\ Get mask and toggle it
   6500AND &0D69           :\ Mask with current baud register copy
   6510STA &F0             :\ Keep the bits we aren't going to change
   6520LDA L84B2,X         :\ Translate X to baud rate control value
   6530AND &F1             :\ Mask to keep the half we are going to change
   6540ORA &F0             :\ Merge with current setting
   6550STA &0D69           :\ Update the baud register RAM copy
   6560STA &FC61           :\ Store in baud register
   6570.L840C
   6580JMP L80D1           :\ Claim call and return
   6600\ OSBYTE &02 - select input stream
   6610\ ================================
   6620.L840F
   6630LDA &F0:PHA         :\ Save OSBYTE X value, input source
   6640AND #&01:TAX        :\ Keep bit 0, keyboard/serial select
   6650STA &0241           :\ Store selected source
   6660PLA:BEQ L8471       :\ Get original selection, exit if X=0, serial disabled
   6670LDA #&01:STA &FC62  :\ Enable serial input
   6680LDA &0D6A:ORA #&02  :\ Enable receive interupts
   6690STA &0D6A:STA &FC65 :\ Write to IRQ mask and 6281 IRQ register
   6700JSR L82EB           :\ Set/Clear CTS
   6710LDA #&10:JSR L85EA  :\ Increment ROM polling register if needed
   6720JMP L80D1           :\ Claim call and return
   6740\ OSBYTE &9C - serial control
   6750\ ===========================
   6760\ On entry, Y=action, X=parameters, in 6850 format
   6770.L8436
   6780LDA #&E3            :\ *FX156,x,227 to set word format
   6790CMP &F1:BNE L8492   :\ Not Y=227
   6810\ Y=227, set serial word format
   6820\ -----------------------------
   6830BIT &F0:BNE L846E   :\ X must be %000xxx00
   6840LDA &F0             :\ Get X parameter
   6850LSR A:LSR A:NOP:TAX :\ Move up two bits
   6860JSR L85A4           :\ Select control register &10
   6870LDA &FC60           :\ Read from control register &10
